## MEDIUM

### POWER-006 — MEDIUM — ANOMALY-004 INTEL: `None` vs `Reactive`
**Merges:** POWER-005 (Medium)
**Location:** `05_ANOMALY_CLASSIFICATION.md:97` · `28_ANOMALIES.md` ANOMALY-004 status line
**Conflicting statements:**
- 05: "ANOMALY-004 'The Counting Stair' — `MV-T1/C-B/—/Local/None`."
- 28: "STATUS: **C-B/—/Local/Reactive**."
**Why it is a problem:** A textbook B-class file — "the kind of file that makes auditors weep with joy" — carries a vector disagreement on its INTEL flag. One-word fix, but it undermines the file's function as the clean example.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. 28 wins (`Reactive` — the stair counts *back*): correct 05.
2. 05 wins (`None`): correct 28's status line.
3. Mark it as an open Classification Office review — but for the textbook example, prefer a clean fix.

### POWER-007 — MEDIUM — The Laggard is filed `C-A` but reads as `C-D`
**Merges:** POWER-007 (Medium)
**Location:** `05_ANOMALY_CLASSIFICATION.md:95` ("ANOMALY-001 'The Laggard' — `MV-T1/C-A/—/Local/Unknown`") · `28_ANOMALIES.md` ANOMALY-001
**Conflicting statements:**
- 05: filed `C-A` — "Filed by BPF Jakarta; the Unknown flag is why the file stays open."
- 28: the Laggard is mobile, person-attached, tracked by passive containment — which reads as `C-D` (the worker's case: a mobile tracked containment is D-class effort, and the filing avoids Compact reporting triggers).
**Why it is a problem:** The filing determines legal triggers: `C-A` avoids the Compact's 72-hour reporting requirement that `C-D` would trip. The worker's finding is that this is a *motivated under-classification* — and the bible never says whether it's deliberate.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Keep `C-A` with an explicit policy line: person-bound anomalies are filed `C-A` by Classification Office policy ("a person cannot be 'uncontained'"), with D-equivalent surveillance — the non-reporting is institutional, not corrupt (see decision (e)).
2. Re-file as `C-D` and explain the buried 72-hour report (heavier: requires a cover-up beat).
3. Add the Classification Office guidance note from POWER-014 option 2 and apply it here.

### POWER-008 — MEDIUM — The Ward Six Lullaby's behavioral compulsion strains "no direct mind control"
**Merges:** POWER-008 (Medium)
**Location:** `28_ANOMALIES.md` ANOMALY-007 · `02_WORLD_RULES.md` (CANON: "No direct mind control; only suggestion seeding with resistance and backlash.")
**Conflicting statements:**
- 28: "A melody that propagates itself and **degrades the singer's memory of having sung it**" — plus the V flag it created: behavioral compulsion to sing.
- 02: "No direct mind control; only suggestion seeding **with resistance and backlash**."
**Why it is a problem:** A melody that compels singing and erases the memory of singing reads as direct behavioral control — the CANON rule's boundary is exactly here, and the bible's most famous memetic case sits on it.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Draw the line explicitly: the Lullaby is *suggestion seeding at scale* — the compulsion admits resistance (the V flag exists because some singers *do* resist and report), and the memory degradation is the backlash mechanism, not mind control.
2. Reclassify the Lullaby as the CANON's boundary case with a Tribunal ruling on which side it falls (a ruling is citable; a handwave isn't).
3. Weaken the compulsion: the melody is *catchy*, not compelling — the propagation is cultural, and the V flag covers only the memory effect.

### POWER-009 — MEDIUM — The Static Saint inflicts Erosion without channeling
**Merges:** POWER-009 (Medium)
**Location:** `28_ANOMALIES.md` ANOMALY-012 · `04_POWER_SYSTEM.md` (Erosion doctrine: Erosion is the cost of *channeling*)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 28: "Anyone who *recognizes* the face… begins to dream of drowning within a week, accrues Erosion at ~1 Fathom per month without channeling, and eventually seeks out deep water."
- 04: "Erosion is irreversible. Channeling wears the Veil" / "Every deliberate channeling wears the user's Veil — measurable as Erosion" — Erosion is the wear-cost paid by the *channeler*, but the Saint's victims aren't channeling anything.
**Why it is a problem:** Either Erosion can be inflicted (which rewrites the Erosion economy — Erosion becomes a weapon, not a cost) or the Saint's effect needs a different name.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Keep the effect and name it: the Saint doesn't inflict *Erosion* — it inflicts *Fathom-debt transfer* or *resonance damage*; reserve "Erosion" for channeling wear (pairs with POWER-010).
2. Make it load-bearing: the Saint is the proof that Erosion *can* be inflicted, and the Compact's Erosion doctrine is wrong — a major plot revelation (expensive; touches 04's CANON).
3. Reclassify the Saint's effect as accelerated *natural* Erosion (the congregation channels unknowingly through the information-seep — they *are* channeling).

### POWER-010 — MEDIUM — The Gilded Cradle reifies Fathoms as transferable objects
**Merges:** POWER-010 (Medium)
**Location:** `28_ANOMALIES.md` ANOMALY-008 · `04_POWER_SYSTEM.md` (Fathoms as a *measure* of Erosion)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 28: "eleven Fathom-debts the world is carrying" — Fathoms treated as transferable ledger entries.
- 04: Fathoms are a *measurement scale* for Erosion (0–100), not a substance.
**Why it is a problem:** The Cradle's Rule (if it survives per decision (f)) treats Fathoms as *things* that can be transferred. Either the ontology or the Rule's description must be fixed.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Clarify the ontology: the Cradle transfers *Erosion* (the wear), and "Fathom-debts" is the Compact's accounting metaphor — the debt language is bureaucratic, not physical.
2. Make the reification real: Fathoms *are* transferable under the Cradle's Rule (an anomaly exceeding Attuned caps with proportional cost — the CANON allows it, but it must be stated).
3. Rewrite the Cradle's Rule to avoid the debt metaphor entirely.

### POWER-011 — MEDIUM — Pre-Drowning T6 containment and doctrine are unspecified
**Merges:** POWER-013 (Medium)
**Location:** `04_POWER_SYSTEM.md` (T6 band) · `05_ANOMALY_CLASSIFICATION.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 04: the T6 band (81–100 Fathoms, "pre-Drowning") is defined as a measurement band.
- (Absence): no file describes what T6 containment looks like, what doctrine governs the pre-Drowned, or what the Compact *does* with someone at 97 Fathoms.
**Why it is a problem:** The scale's most dangerous band has no institutional response. Arthur's ratchet climbs *toward* T6 — the endgame's physics has no doctrine.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Write the T6 doctrine: mandatory relief rotation, the Drowning watch protocol, what happens at 100 (Sweeper standby?) — one subsection in 04.
2. Keep it unspecified deliberately: the Compact has no T6 doctrine because no one survives to need one (the Drowning is the doctrine) — state the absence as CANON.
3. Tie T6 to the Worldtide level: pre-Drowning cases are escalated to Level 7-adjacent handling (but then the Level-7 silence rules apply).

### PROTAG-001 — MEDIUM — The ratchet math is unbounded and self-referential
**Merges:** PROTAG-001 (Moderate) · SER-003 (Moderate) · RED-016 (Medium)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` §B.6 (ratchet clause) · `27_SERIALIZATION_GUIDE.md` §27.4
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: "Each **logged** Use deepens the lag by ~0.25s; each **unlogged** Use deepens it by ~1s… the only ceiling is **10 seconds** — and **nobody knows what happens at 10s**."
- 27: the serialization plan paces the ratchet across seasons but never states the season-by-season budget.
- RED-016's worker finding: the math as written compounds without a stated cap-per-season, and the 10s ceiling's unknown is doing structural work it can't do forever.
**Why it is a problem:** The novel's central clock has no published face: the reader can't feel the ratchet tightening if the increments aren't legible, and the 10s unknown is a series-ending mystery being spent as a season-level mechanic.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Publish the drafter's ruler (non-binding): ~0.25s logged / ~1s unlogged, with a per-season lag budget — marked as guideline, not hard canon (see decision (k)).
2. Keep the math diegetic-only: Arthur doesn't know the increments either, and the bible tracks them in a hidden ledger (35) the reader never sees — the *feel* is managed by the author, not the numbers.
3. Cap the ratchet's narrative role: the 10s ceiling is a series-level mystery (L5-adjacent) and seasons are paced by *Fathoms*, not lag-seconds.

### PROTAG-003 — MEDIUM — The flare's point-of-no-return needs a permanent consequence
**Merges:** SER-005 (Moderate) · RED-015 (Medium)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` (flare rules) · `27_SERIALIZATION_GUIDE.md` §27.7
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: the flare "burns the filing" — but the T1-curiosity filing's *reinstatement* mechanics are also described, implying the burn isn't permanent.
- 27: the flare is staged as the season's point-of-no-return.
**Why it is a problem:** A point-of-no-return that can be returned from is a fake climax. The worker's finding: the flare must permanently burn the T1-curiosity filing, or the season's hinge doesn't hold.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Make the burn permanent: the T1-curiosity filing is gone forever; Arthur's cover story must be rebuilt from scratch (the rebuild is the next season's engine).
2. Keep reinstatement but cost it: the filing can be reinstated only through a named, expensive, Compact-level intervention (a favor Arthur can't afford — functionally permanent, technically reversible).
3. Move the point-of-no-return to a different beat and downgrade the flare to a major setback.

### PROTAG-004 — MEDIUM — The sterile-kill denial creates an unsatisfying immunity
**Merges:** PROTAG-002 (Moderate)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` §C (sterile-kill doctrine)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: Arthur *cannot* be sterile-killed (the tether's transfer mechanics make a clean kill impossible to arrange without dissipating the tether — which is what a sterile-kill wants to prevent).
- 29: the bible also maintains that Arthur survives by observation and cunning, "without plot armor."
**Why it is a problem:** An unkillable protagonist is plot armor by physics. The denial needs to cost more than it protects.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Keep the denial but price it: every failed sterile-kill *deepens* the lag (the tether notices) — survival is purchased with ratchet progress.
2. Narrow the denial: sterile-kill fails only *while the tether is stable*; destabilize the tether and he's mortal (the Quiet Desk's research goal becomes destabilization, not assassination).
3. Remove the denial: he's killable, and the institutions' restraint is political (the escrow), not physical.

### PROTAG-005 — MEDIUM — Detection radius per use-tier is unstated
**Merges:** RED-001 (Medium)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` §B (the twelve uses) · `07_INTERNATIONAL_RELATIONS.md` (Seismograph)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: the uses are tiered by lag-depth, but no detection radius is stated per tier.
- 07: the Seismograph "sees large Tide events" — the threshold for "large" is never quantified against the use-tiers.
**Why it is a problem:** The stealth calculus — Arthur's core survival mechanic — can't be planned if neither the author nor the reader knows which uses the Seismograph sees.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Publish the radius table: per-tier detection radius vs. Seismograph / Lantern / witness thresholds (a drafter's ruler, non-binding).
2. Keep it vague but principled: state the *rule* (deeper lag = larger signature, with the Membranous phase always visible) without numbers.
3. Make the uncertainty diegetic: Arthur doesn't know the radii either, and the bible tracks them in the hidden ledger.

### PROTAG-006 — MEDIUM — The smiling mirror reflection is an unregistered anomaly
**Merges:** RED-002 (Medium)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` §A (mirror beat)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: "His mirror reflection sometimes smiles when he isn't — documented by Ilsa, observed by Arthur twice."
- 33: it appears in no mystery registry, no Rule file, and no plot-thread list — it's documented but unregistered.
**Why it is a problem:** A documented supernatural event with no institutional trace is either a plot thread (register it) or a continuity error (explain the missing trace).
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Register it: add it to 33 as an L4/L5-adjacent mystery or to 31 as a plot thread with an owner.
2. Explain the gap: the reflection was never reported (Arthur told no one) — the absence of a trace *is* the trace, and the beat's secrecy becomes character-relevant.
3. Cut the beat (cheapest, but it may be load-bearing for the Laggard's nature — check before cutting).

### PROTAG-007 — MEDIUM — Use-10's rot-immunity boundary is unstated
**Merges:** RED-004 (Medium)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` §B (Use 10) · `26_INFORMATION_CONTROL.md` (static rot)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: Use 10 grants rot-immunity (the replay doesn't decay).
- 26: "Static rot is proportional to event intensity" — and deep-lag/high-pressure replay is the highest-intensity event class in the bible.
**Why it is a problem:** The immunity's boundary is exactly where the physics is most stressed: does rot-immunity hold for deep-lag replay under high pressure? The bible doesn't say, and the answer determines whether Use 10 is a tool or a WMD.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. State the boundary: immunity holds up to a stated lag-depth/pressure product, beyond which rot applies normally (a number the author can plan around).
2. Make the boundary unknown *in-world*: the Compact's rot doctrine has no data on deep-lag replay (Arthur is the experiment — the uncertainty is the plot).
3. Remove the immunity: Use 10 replays rot like everything else, and Arthur's edge is *knowing* the rot rate (information advantage, not physics advantage).

### PROTAG-008 — MEDIUM — Use-10 needs doctrinal reconciliation with the anti-Snowden regime
**Merges:** RED-008 (Medium)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` §B (Use 10) · `26_INFORMATION_CONTROL.md` (the anti-Snowden / static-rot regime)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: Use 10 lets Arthur replay events — functionally, a perfect-recall capability.
- 26: the Compact's anti-Snowden regime exists *because* perfect recall is a civilizational threat — it targets "witnessed, multi-sensor, in-person *or otherwise non-rotting*" evidence ("could clean evidence exist?" — "theory: yes; observed: never").
**Why it is a problem:** Use 10 is the thing the whole information-control doctrine exists to prevent. The bible needs to say why the regime tolerates (or hasn't noticed) a walking Snowden.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. The regime *hasn't* noticed: Use 10's signature is below the Seismograph's threshold (pairs with PROTAG-005's radius table).
2. The regime has noticed and the Quiet Desk's interest in Arthur *is* the response — the doctrine is working as designed, on him.
3. Use 10's replay is lossy in a way the bible hasn't stated (the replay degrades like a copy of a copy — rot applies to the *viewer*, not the record).

### POL-001 — MEDIUM — Aisha's contracting authority is split across two agencies
**Merges:** POL-002 (Moderate) · LAW-005 (Moderate)
**Location:** `06_GOVERNMENTS.md` (Aisha's profile) · `14_LAW_ENFORCEMENT.md` (contracting authority)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 06: "Aisha Rahman (b. 1988) — **GOV-006-contracted** Sweeper team lead, Ravenscroft; Filipina-Muslim; **formerly KNF Liwanag (recruited 2023)**."
- 14: her contracting authority is attributed to a different agency's procurement chain.
**Why it is a problem:** The character who signs the contracts must have a single, valid warrant. Two agencies means neither warrant is clean — every contract she touches is challengeable.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Pick the surviving agency and rewrite the other file's attribution (one-line fix once decided).
2. Make the split deliberate: she's dual-hatted (liaison authority), and the dual warrant is the point — but then the files must *say* she's dual-hatted.
3. Move her to the Compact's procurement (neutral ground) — but that changes her institutional loyalty.

### POL-004 — MEDIUM — The 3-year director rotation vs. the NPA turf war
**Merges:** POL-005 (Moderate)
**Location:** `06_GOVERNMENTS.md` (SMD director rotation)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 06: the SMD director rotates every 3 years (a reform safeguard) — but the NPA turf war is "the 1995 charter's original sin," a structural, decades-long conflict "litigated constantly."
**Why it is a problem:** The safeguard designed to prevent entrenchment also prevents the sustained leadership the turf war's resolution would require. Either the rotation or the war's framing must adjust.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Keep the rotation and make the turf war *bureaucratic*, not personal: the conflict is between institutions, and directors inherit it (the war outlives every director — which is the point).
2. Lengthen the rotation (5–7 years) for the director while keeping 3-year rotations below.
3. Exempt the Ravenscroft field office from the rotation (field continuity vs. headquarters turnover — a new tension).

### POL-007 — MEDIUM — GOV-015 and GOV-016 are stubs
**Merges:** RED-006 (Medium)
**Location:** `06_GOVERNMENTS.md` (GOV-015, GOV-016) · `35_CANON_DATABASE.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 06: GOV-015/016 exist as registry entries reading "Content to be developed."
- 35: the database lists them as if they were real entries.
**Why it is a problem:** Stub entries in the government registry — the audit suggests the intended content (SMD charter details; Albion accession terms) may already be covered by 06's main profile, making the stubs pure residue.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Delist the stubs (if 06's profile already covers the content).
2. Write minimal entries (if the content isn't covered elsewhere).
3. Merge them into adjacent entries (GOV-015 into the SMD profile, GOV-016 into the Compact-Albion accession note).

### MIL-001 — MEDIUM — No anomaly-war doctrine
**Merges:** MIL-001 (Moderate)
**Location:** `09_MILITARY.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 09: the file describes forces, arsenals (Drowndust), and thresholds — but no doctrine for *fighting* an anomaly (as opposed to containing one).
- 04: anomalies "may exceed Attuned caps only with proportional costs" — the military implications are never drawn.
**Why it is a problem:** The bible's militaries have weapons (Drowndust), tripwires (the 90-second rule), and no war plan. The Saratov precedent (MIL-006) shows what happens without one.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Write the doctrine: containment-first, Drowndust as last resort, the 90-second rule's escalation ladder — one section in 09.
2. Keep the gap deliberate: the Compact *forbids* anomaly-war doctrine (the 1949 Accords' core bargain), and the absence is the policy — state it.
3. Give one power a secret doctrine (the US BTA's, per the Roswell lineage) and make the asymmetry the plot.

### MIL-002 — MEDIUM — Drowndust deterrence has no stated target set or supply
**Merges:** MIL-004 (Moderate)
**Location:** `09_MILITARY.md` (Drowndust arsenal)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 09: Drowndust is the strategic deterrent — "**Quiet people are unaffected** — the dust forces *Fathom accumulation*, and the Quiet have no Hollow Pattern to accumulate in; it is a weapon that works only on the Attuned" — but no file states what it's *for* (which anomalies? which factions?) or where it *comes from* (banned since 1977, no licit production — cf. FAC-010).
**Why it is a problem:** A deterrent with no target set deters nothing; a banned substance with no supply chain is a continuity error (or a crime — cf. FAC-010's stockpile question).
**Proposed solutions:**
1. State both: the target set (named E-class contingencies) and the supply (pre-1977 caches + the Saratov precedent's leftovers), with custody stated.
2. Make the deterrent a bluff: the arsenals are smaller than claimed (pairs with FAC-010 option 2).
3. Give Drowndust a licit synthesis path under Compact control (a CANON change — expensive).

### MIL-003 — MEDIUM — Tide-noise vs. witnessed combat
**Merges:** MIL-005 (Moderate)
**Location:** `09_MILITARY.md` · `26_INFORMATION_CONTROL.md` (Veil doctrine)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 09: "Tide-noise generators. Area-denial systems that flood the spectrum with artificial Tide pressure — **jamming both channeling and Seismograph detection**… standard cover for covert Attuned operations."
- 26: the Veil doctrine assumes large-scale Quiet combat *can* be concealed — but if combat produces Seismograph-visible Tide-noise ("witnessed combat"), the noise-farming cover stories must explain Seismograph-grade events.
**Why it is a problem:** The military's signature and the Veil's promise collide: either big fights are visible (and the cover stories must explain Seismograph-grade events) or they're concealable (and the noise doctrine is wrong).
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Draw the line: tactical engagements are concealable (noise-farming covers them); *strategic* Threshold events are not (the Seismograph sees them, and the cover story is "earthquake/explosion" — the Batavia Static precedent).
2. Make the collision the plot: a recent engagement *was* seen, and the cover story is failing (EVENT-092's media entries hint at this).
3. Upgrade noise-farming: the Compact has a dedicated capability for laundering Seismograph-grade signatures (but then say what it costs).

### LAW-002 — MEDIUM — Arthur's situation is the sharp edge of the legal framework
**Merges:** LAW-003 (Moderate)
**Location:** `14_LAW_ENFORCEMENT.md` · `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 14: the legal framework is complete and self-consistent — *except* where Arthur's tether touches it: the law "cannot sever the tether" (the trafficking premise misdescribes the physics per LAW-001, the succession vacuum, the C-A filing's administrative convenience).
- 29: Arthur's existence is the case the law can't process.
**Why it is a problem:** (Not a contradiction — a load-bearing design note the audit surfaced.) The law's completeness *breaking* at the protagonist is the theme; the risk is a future patch "fixing" it by over-legislating.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Canonize the principle: "the law is complete except where the tether touches it" — a named doctrine (the *Arthur exception* as jurisprudence, not statute).
2. Leave it implicit (current state) — but then LAW-001's acknowledgment clause must be carefully worded not to close the gap.
3. Close the gap deliberately in a later volume (the Tribunal's CASE-007 as the vehicle — but that ends the theme).

### INFO-001 — MEDIUM — The SSW watchlist problem
**Merges:** INFO-003 (Moderate)
**Location:** `26_INFORMATION_CONTROL.md` · `17_MEDIA_SOCIETY.md` (SSW program)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 26: the information-control regime watches foreign workers as a matter of doctrine.
- 17: the SSW program's scale (tens of thousands, "compiled from dispatch-company filings") makes individual watching impossible — the doctrine and the capacity don't match.
**Why it is a problem:** The doctrine promises surveillance the institutions can't deliver. Either the doctrine is aspirational (and should say so) or the capacity exists (and should be shown).
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Tier the watchlist: only SSW workers in Threshold-adjacent jobs are watched (which is *why* Arthur's records-clerk job matters — it's Threshold-adjacent by placement, per decision (d)).
2. Make the gap load-bearing: the Compact *knows* it can't watch everyone, and the SSW program is the Veil's known blind spot (a plot engine).
3. Automate it: the watchlist is algorithmic (pattern flags, not human watchers) — state the false-positive rate.

### TECH-001 — MEDIUM — Commercial satellites and drones vs. persistent sites
**Merges:** TECH-001 (Moderate) · TECH-006 (Low, folded per worker note)
**Location:** `19_TECHNOLOGY.md` · `26_INFORMATION_CONTROL.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 19: "the few civilian researchers who've noticed the *gaps* (seismologists, mostly) get visited by polite people with publication advice" — but commercial satellite imagery and civilian drones are ubiquitous, and persistent sites (the Salt Garden, the Ravenscroft Wound) are geographically fixed and long-lived.
**Why it is a problem:** The Veil's hardest problem in 2024 isn't witnesses — it's pixels. The bible's concealment doctrine predates the satellite constellation era and never updated.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Update the doctrine: the Compact's concealment stack includes satellite-tasking influence, imagery-laundering, and drone no-fly enforcement (state the cost — it's the Veil's most expensive program).
2. Make the sites *unphotographable*: the anomalies themselves degrade imagery (a Rule-level property — but that's a new power, price it).
3. Make the gap the plot: a civilian satellite *has* imaged a site, and the cover-up is failing (EVENT-092's media thread).

### TECH-002 — MEDIUM — Open LLMs vs. the Quiet
**Merges:** TECH-003 (Moderate)
**Location:** `19_TECHNOLOGY.md` · `26_INFORMATION_CONTROL.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 19: "> CANON: **ML proposes Rule hypotheses; humans confirm them**… **no model trains on unreviewed Seep-adjacent text**."
- 26: the information-control regime assumes controllable chokepoints (publishers, platforms, the Gray Book's editors) — but open-weight LLMs run locally, offline, by anyone.
**Why it is a problem:** A local LLM trained on leaked Quiet data is an unseizable printing press. The regime's chokepoint model doesn't cover open weights.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Extend the regime: the Compact's information doctrine covers model weights (poisoned datasets, the "deepfake dividend" as countermeasure — 02 already says it "cuts both ways").
2. Make open LLMs the Gray Market's press: the Quiet's samizdat runs on local models, and the Compact's response (model poisoning) is the arms race.
3. Scope it out: LLMs can't help — the Quiet's knowledge is *embodied* (Lantern examination, Tide-sense), not textual, so text models are useless (but then say why the Gray Book matters).

### TECH-003 — MEDIUM — The Obsidian 2019 clean recording
**Merges:** TECH-005 (Moderate)
**Location:** `19_TECHNOLOGY.md` (Obsidian incident, 2019)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 19: a clean recording of a Threshold event was made in 2019 (the Obsidian incident) — though 19 also holds that "Sensors are *worse* witnesses than people."
- 26: the static-rot doctrine says clean recordings shouldn't survive (rot is proportional to intensity).
**Why it is a problem:** A clean recording is either a doctrine-breaking event (generalizable — then rot doctrine falls) or a contained one-off (then say why it can't be repeated and who holds the recording).
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Contain it: the recording was a fluke of specific conditions (stated), it's in Compact custody, and the conditions are unrepeatable — a registered exception.
2. Generalize it: the Compact *can* make clean recordings now, and the information regime has already adapted (the adaptation is the story — 26 needs the update).
3. Destroy it: the recording decayed (rot caught up — the doctrine holds, the incident is a lesson).

### FAC-011 — MEDIUM — The Cartographers' 28-year inaction on Ilsa's journals
**Merges:** RED-007 (Medium)
**Location:** `12_FACTIONS.md` (Cartographers) · `33_MYSTERIES.md` (L5-5: Ilsa Brandt's torn final page)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 12: Ilsa's journals are "held by the Cartographers' Exchange" — 28 years, status "dormant — monitor."
- 33: L5-5 (the torn final page) is an *active* top-level mystery — but no file explains why 28 years of inaction ended *now* ("Their interest is scientific, which makes it the most dangerous kind: they will *experiment*").
**Why it is a problem:** A 28-year cold case needs a "why now?" — without it, the mystery's activation is authorial timing, not causality.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Supply the trigger: a recent event (the Ravenscroft pressure rise? the Laggard's market listing?) made the journals relevant — state which.
2. The inaction was the action: the Cartographers were *waiting* for a specific condition (the torn page's subject to reappear), and the condition just obtained.
3. They weren't inactive: 28 years of quiet research failed, and the failure is documented (the dead ends are in the file).

### GEO-002 — MEDIUM — Ravenscroft sits on two bays
**Merges:** GEO-002 (Medium)
**Location:** `20_GEOGRAPHY.md` (Ravenscroft: "on Enshū-nada" vs "on Suruga Bay")
**Conflicting statements:**
- 20: Ravenscroft is "~8.9M people on the Enshū-nada coast of Ravenshire Prefecture, central Honshu" in one passage — but "## LOC-007 — The Listening Shore (Suruga Bay, Ravenscroft)" and "## LOC-020 — The Ravenscroft Anchorage (Suruga Bay)" place it on Suruga Bay.
**Why it is a problem:** A city can't front two bays unless it straddles the cape between them. The bible never says it does.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Make it canonical: Ravenscroft straddles Cape Omaezaki, fronting Enshū-nada to the south and Suruga Bay to the east — the cape geography becomes a feature (two waterfronts, two smuggling routes).
2. Pick one bay and fix the other passage.
3. Make the confusion in-world: the "two bays" are a cartographic dispute (but that's lore debt for a copy error — prefer 1).

### GEO-003 — MEDIUM — ZONE-006 "Odaiba Wound" is in Ravenshire
**Merges:** GEO-003 (Medium)
**Location:** `20_GEOGRAPHY.md` (ZONE-006)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 20: ZONE-006 is "the Odaiba Wound" — located in Ravenshire.
- (Real-world geography, reader-facing): Odaiba is a famous Tokyo waterfront district.
**Why it is a problem:** Any Albion-literate reader trips on a Tokyo place-name in Ravenshire. The name breaks immersion on contact.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Rename to a Ravenshire-local name (see decision (g)).
2. Keep it as an in-world nickname with a stated reason (who named it, why) — lore debt for no gain; not recommended.
3. Move the zone to Tokyo Bay (but the zone registry is Ravenscroft-centric — expensive).

### GEO-004 — MEDIUM — The 8.9M Ravenscroft metro vs. real Ravenshire geography
**Merges:** GEO-004 (Medium)
**Location:** `20_GEOGRAPHY.md` (Ravenscroft metro: 8.9M)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 20: Ravenscroft metro is 8.9M people — "incorporating the Hamamatsu and Ravenshire City urban areas" after "the post-war Showa mergers consolidated half a dozen fishing and port towns into a single bay city."
- (Real-world geography): Ravenshire Prefecture is ~3.6M; Hamamatsu ~800k — an 8.9M metro doesn't fit the real coastline.
**Why it is a problem:** The fictional city's scale contradicts the real prefecture it's embedded in. Readers who check a map find the seam.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Reconcile explicitly: the Ravenscroft metro *absorbed* Hamamatsu/Ravenshire into a fictional conurbation (state the merger history — the 8.9M is then honest worldbuilding).
2. Shrink Ravenscroft to fit (2–3M) and rescale dependent numbers (the port, the SSW population, the metro-wide plots).
3. Move Ravenscroft offshore of the real geography: it's a fictional *bay* the real maps don't show (but the bays are real — prefer 1).

### SOC-002 — MEDIUM — The "three-second rule" is defined as 180 seconds
**Merges:** SOC-002 (Moderate)
**Location:** `24_HISTORY.md:114` · `25_TIMEmessaging app.md:469` · `31_CONFLICT_ENGINE.md:16, 513, 592, 696`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 24:114 / 25:469: "the 'three-second rule' (any livestreamed Seep gets 180 seconds before takedown, because static rot usually kills it first)."
- 31:16: "The 180-second livestream rule ([EVENT-080](25_TIMEmessaging app.md)) starts ticking." / 31:513: "the three-second rule fails at fiesta scale."
**Why it is a problem:** The rule is *named* "three-second" and *defined* as 180 seconds (three minutes) — in the same parenthetical, twice. The conflict engine then uses "the 180-second livestream rule," "the 180-second rule," and "the three-second rule" interchangeably. A rule whose name contradicts its own definition by a factor of 60 reads as a drafting error — nothing in canon justifies the misnomer.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Rename to the "three-minute rule" everywhere (matches the 180-second definition).
2. Keep "three-second rule" as in-world slang and add the one-line justification (e.g., named for the three seconds a spotter has to *start* the protocol) — but then the parenthetical definition needs rewriting, not the name.
3. Split them: the "three-second rule" (spotter's initiation window) and the "180-second rule" (takedown window) are two different rules — but the current text uses them for the same doctrine, so the split needs evidence.

### NAR-003 — MEDIUM — Pram's destination: Jakarta vs Ravenscroft
**Merges:** NAR-001 (Moderate) · FAC-011 (Minor) · RED-005 (Medium)
**Location:** `32_NARRATIVE_THREADS.md` §32.2 · `25_TIMEmessaging app.md` · `12_FACTIONS.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 32: "Pramudya Nugroho (CHAR-032) has been sent to Jakarta ([EVENT-100](25_TIMEmessaging app.md))."
- 12: "**Hidden truth:** Pramudya Nugroho (CHAR-032), Cartographer (SUP-002), found Ilsa Brandt's 1996 field map in the Exchange… Pram is sent to Ravenscroft" — "reassigned to Ravenscroft ([EVENT-100](25_TIMEmessaging app.md)), arriving September 2024."
**Why it is a problem:** A named character's location is single-valued. The relocation half-moved him.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Fix the chain: Pram goes to Jakarta *first*, then Ravenscroft (the journey is the beat — two destinations, one trip).
2. Pick one destination and fix the other file.
3. Make the ambiguity load-bearing: Pram's *reported* destination differs from his *actual* one (he's being moved quietly — but then say who's moving him).

### NAR-004 — MEDIUM — CG-044's BPF/KNF mislabel
**Merges:** NAR-002 (Moderate) · RED-014 (Medium)
**Location:** `31_CONFLICT_ENGINE.md` (CG-044) · `35_CANON_DATABASE.md` (CG-044 row)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 31: "### CG-044 — The BPF Mole Hunt" — "Setup: Someone inside BPF (GOV-004) is selling Sweeper patrol schedules…"
- 35: CG-044's filing agency is stated as KNF in the 35 row.
**Why it is a problem:** The filing agency determines jurisdiction (BPF Jakarta vs KNF national). A mislabeled filing sends the plot to the wrong office.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Pick the agency (KNF, if it's the national file; BPF, if it's the Jakarta incident file) and fix the other.
2. Make it a *transferred* file: BPF originated it, KNF absorbed it (the transfer is the beat — the absorption is why the Quiet Desk has it).
3. Add a filing-agency authority table to 35 so the error class can't recur.

### SER-002 — MEDIUM — The five romance configurations need a pre-serialization choice
**Merges:** SER-004 (Moderate) · RED-019 (Medium)
**Location:** `32_ROMANCE_FRAMEWORK.md` §32.2 · `32_NARRATIVE_THREADS.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 32: "Configs A–E are each built for 100–200 chapter arcs with structural (not miscommunication) obstacles" — five live configurations, with Config E (Saitō) doubling as the Loud-politics thread (CG-017/041, RC-018/030) and Config A (Nadia) the only Quiet option.
- 32: the narrative threads assume romantic *availability* without committing to a configuration.
**Why it is a problem:** The configurations imply different character arcs, different Loud-politics entanglements (Config E doubles as a Loud-politics thread), and different Veil risks. Serialization can't start while all five are live.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Choose the primary configuration before chapter 1 (the audit's recommendation — see decision (i)).
2. Keep all five as *framework* (canon-neutral) and defer the choice to the serialization plan, not the patch phase.
3. Merge configurations: the "choice" is the season-1 arc (but that spends a season on a decision the bible should have made).

### REL-004 — MEDIUM — REL-008 has no profile but is staged by the conflict engine
**Merges:** REL-003 (Medium)
**Location:** `12_FACTIONS.md` (REL-008: stub) · `31_CONFLICT_ENGINE.md:513`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 12: REL-008 is a stub — but 31:513 already stages it: "| REL-008 | Church of the Open Eye | Seep-venerating revelationists; Tide-marks as scripture |" / "the Open Eye formally claims the event as doctrine and the Veil excommunicates the claimants."
**Why it is a problem:** A faction with staged beats but no profile is a ghost actor — the beats' motivations are unreadable.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Write the REL-008 profile (short — the beats already imply its doctrine; see decision (b)).
2. Cut the 31:513 beats (if REL-008 is delisted as a reserved namespace).
3. Fold REL-008 into an existing faith faction's profile as a sub-order.

## LOW

### POWER-012 — LOW — "Routine" Erosion rates vs T2 career viability
**Merges:** POWER-011 (Low)
**Location:** `04_POWER_SYSTEM.md` (routine Erosion rates) · `06_GOVERNMENTS.md` (SMD staffing)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 04: "Sustainable career: 15–25 years of professional work at discipline hygiene." / "Life expectancy: Near-normal with care."
- 04: routine channeling accrues Erosion at rates where "career T2s Drown from routine work" — compounded over a career, most T2 operators would Drown before retirement.
**Why it is a problem:** The arithmetic says the workforce shouldn't exist. Either the rates or the careers are wrong.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Publish the relief-rotation math: scheduled stand-downs keep career totals under the Drowning line (the depth-pay incentives in ECON-012 then price the residual risk).
2. Lower the routine rates (a CANON-adjacent change — the rates are soft canon, so this is cheap).
3. Make short careers the norm: T2 Lanterns burn out in 8–12 years, and the precariat gap (06) is partly a *retirement* crisis.

### POWER-013 — LOW — EVENT-047's one-line database entry contradicts the full timeline
**Merges:** POWER-012 (Low) · (the phantom "POWER-015" from PROTAGONIST_AUDIT.md Part 8 is a typo for this issue)
**Location:** `25_TIMEmessaging app.md` (EVENT-047 full entry) · `35_CANON_DATABASE.md` (EVENT-047 one-liner)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 25: EVENT-047 is "a global pressure spike with no Choir actor."
- 35: the one-liner reads "T6 Choir event."
**Why it is a problem:** The database invents a Choir actor the timeline explicitly excludes — the phantom-actor class of error.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Fix the 35 one-liner to match the timeline (mechanical).
2. Add a 35 validation rule: one-liners may not introduce actors absent from the source entry.
3. (Nuclear option, not recommended): canonize a Choir actor — contradicts the timeline's explicit exclusion.

### POWER-014 — LOW — Vector notation mismatches between 05.5 and 28
**Merges:** POWER-014 (Low)
**Location:** `05_ANOMALY_CLASSIFICATION.md` §5.5 · `28_ANOMALIES.md` (older notes, e.g. "MV-T1/C-A/—/Local/Reactive")
**Conflicting statements:**
- 05: the current vector grammar — "Notation: `MV-[THREAT]/[CONTAINMENT]-[FLAGS]/[RANGE]/[INTEL]`."
- 28: older anomaly notes use a previous revision, e.g. "The filed `MV-T1/D/Local/Reactive` reading that circulates in older examiner notes…"
**Why it is a problem:** Copy-level inconsistency in the classification strings — the kind of error that makes drafters mistrust the registry.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Normalize all 28 vectors to the 05.5 grammar (mechanical pass).
2. Add a Classification Office guidance note *in-world*: older notes use the pre-revision grammar, and the revision history is part of the bureaucracy's texture (pairs with POWER-007 option 3).

### PROTAG-009 — LOW — The escrow deters only rational actors
**Merges:** PROTAG-004 (Moderate)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` §C.9
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: "A dead man's switch only deters **rational** actors. The Cartographers' Quiet Desk is rational… So is everyone else with a filing cabinet."
- 29: the Choir (the least rational actor) is the faction most likely to move against Arthur directly.
**Why it is a problem:** The deterrent's stated limitation is also its central vulnerability — and the bible never prices the irrational-actor case.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. State the residual risk explicitly: the escrow buys safety from institutions, not from fanatics — Arthur's Choir-facing precautions are a separate system (and should be shown).
2. Extend the deterrent: the cache includes Choir-specific material (their Drowndust sourcing — cf. FAC-010), making even the Choir calculate.
3. Accept the gap as theme: rationality is the Quiet world's currency, and the Choir's irrationality is why they're the antagonists.

### PROTAG-010 — LOW — "Engineered transfer" intent is fuzzy at the boundary
**Merges:** PROTAG-005 (Moderate)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` §B.8 ("Transfer cannot be engineered… if the nearest Loud witness was *positioned* rather than present")
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: positioning a witness voids the transfer — but "positioned rather than present" has no boundary: is hiring Arthur as a records clerk (decision (d)) "positioning"?
**Why it is a problem:** The novel's central employment beat (someone wanted him inside) sits exactly on the engineered-transfer boundary. The bible must say whether the hiring voids a future transfer.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Draw the line: *proximate* positioning (arranging the death scene) voids; *life* positioning (employment, housing) doesn't — the tether distinguishes the death from the biography.
2. Make it ambiguous in-world: no one knows where the line is, and the Quiet Desk's research program is trying to find out (plot engine).
3. Tighten it: any deliberate placement voids — which makes Arthur's employment a transfer-voiding act (major plot consequence; choose deliberately).

### PROTAG-011 — LOW — Use-10's Membrane-darkness single anchor
**Merges:** PROTAG-006 (Moderate)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` §B (Use 10)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: "The shadow is Membrane-darkness, not Undertow-structured information — so **recordings of the shadow do not rot**."
- 29: Use 10's Membranous-phase replay is anchored to a single darkness event — but the anchor's stability across deepening lag is never addressed.
**Why it is a problem:** A single anchor for a deepening-lag replay is a single point of failure the bible never acknowledges.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Acknowledge it: the anchor degrades with lag-depth, and Use 10 gets riskier as the ratchet climbs (the endgame's physics prices itself).
2. Multiply the anchors: Arthur establishes backup anchors (a training beat).
3. Remove the anchor requirement (but then Use 10's limits need a different foundation).

### PROTAG-012 — LOW — Misconceptions #2 and #5 frame the tether inconsistently
**Merges:** PROTAG-007 (Low)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` §D (misconceptions list)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: "> STATUS: IN-WORLD MISCONCEPTION (all of the following are wrong; all are believed by someone)" — but misconception #2 and #5 describe the tether's nature in mutually inconsistent terms (one implies the tether is *aware*, the other that it's *mechanical*; the worker flags #2 as "the most structurally dangerous").
**Why it is a problem:** The misconceptions list is the reader's calibration tool — it can't contradict itself.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Rewrite one of the two for consistency (mechanical).
2. Keep both and mark the inconsistency as *in-world* disagreement (two schools of tether theory — but the list is supposed to be authorial, not in-world).

### PROTAG-013 — LOW — The 2023 biennial monitor: deferred or completed?
**Merges:** RED-009 (Low)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md` (2023 monitor) · `25_TIMEmessaging app.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: the Quiet monitor runs "biennially" — but neither 29 nor 25 records the 2023 outcome; the red team notes the "protection expiring" logic depends on it.
**Why it is a problem:** An unexamined examination is a paper-trail gap — the Quiet Desk either cleared him (why?) or never examined him (why not?).
**Proposed solutions:**
1. The exam was deferred (austerity backlog — RC-040's cost-cutting) — the deferral feeds the "protection expiring" plot (see decision (j)).
2. The exam happened and he passed (the tether reads as T1-curiosity at low lag — the system worked, which is worse for everyone).
3. The exam happened and the results were *suppressed* (someone wanted him inside — pairs with decision (d)).

### PROTAG-014 — LOW — "Membrane" vs "Membranous" terminology
**Merges:** RED-010 (Low)
**Location:** `29_PROTAGONIST_ANOMALY.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 29: "Membrane" and "Membranous" are used interchangeably for the phase-state.
**Why it is a problem:** Terminology drift in the novel's central mechanic.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Standardize on one term (mechanical find-and-replace).
2. Differentiate: "Membrane" (the phenomenon) vs "Membranous" (the phase-state adjective) — but the current usage doesn't observe the distinction.

### POL-002 — LOW — Lantern-examiner loan direction
**Merges:** POL-003 (Low) · LAW-004 (Low)
**Location:** `06_GOVERNMENTS.md` (Ravenshire liaison friction) · `14_LAW_ENFORCEMENT.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 06: "the Ravenscroft field office borrows the prefectural quiet desk's Lantern examiners and never returns them on time."
- 14: the loan is described as flowing the other direction (prefectural desk borrowing from the Division).
**Why it is a problem:** The turf-war's central grievance points both ways. The joke only works in one direction.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. 06 wins (the Division borrows and doesn't return — the prefectural grievance is the point); fix 14.
2. 14 wins; fix 06 (but the "never returns them on time" beat is the better joke — prefer 1).

### POL-003 — LOW — Albion's 1949 signatory status is ambiguous
**Merges:** POL-001 (Low)
**Location:** `06_GOVERNMENTS.md` · `24_HISTORY.md` (1949 Accords signatories)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 24: the 1949 signatory list's treatment of Albion (under occupation) is ambiguous — "forty-one states and the Vatican" with Albion's status unclear.
- 06: the SMD's origin as a "US occupation-supervised desk" implies Albion wasn't a full signatory.
**Why it is a problem:** The primary setting country's founding relationship to the Compact is unclear — it affects the SMD's legitimacy narrative.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. State it: Albion signed as an occupied power under SCAP countersignature (the supervised-desk origin follows naturally).
2. Albion didn't sign in 1949 and acceded later (a stated accession date becomes part of GOV-016's content — pairs with POL-007).
3. Leave ambiguous deliberately (but the ambiguity currently reads as an oversight, not a mystery).

### POL-005 — LOW — The SMD "studied the KNF dynamic"
**Merges:** POL-006 (Low)
**Location:** `06_GOVERNMENTS.md` (Blackwater Syndicate truce: "the Division's planners modeled it on the KNF's Daungan understanding")
**Conflicting statements:**
- 06: "the field chief is the Division's real operational head in all but title — a dynamic the Division's planners studied in the KNF's Liwanag office with professional interest."
- (Absence): no file describes the study, the liaison, or what was actually borrowed.
**Why it is a problem:** A cross-agency borrowing with no paper trail — the kind of detail that either needs one line or should be cut.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Add the line: a named liaison exchange (the KNF's Daungan file, shared under a stated agreement).
2. Cut the borrowing: the SMD invented the truce independently (the parallel is convergent evolution, not study).
3. Make the borrowing deniable: the "study" was informal (an officer's memoir), and the KNF was never credited — a small diplomatic sore.

### POL-006 — LOW — Great-power incentive structure is underspecified
**Merges:** POL-007 (Low)
**Location:** `07_INTERNATIONAL_RELATIONS.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 07: "Everything the Meridian Compact achieves, it achieves because its members find compliance cheaper than defiance" — but no file states what each great power *gets* (the US, China, and Russia have different Quiet interests).
**Why it is a problem:** The Compact's stability is asserted, not motivated. A hegemonic bargain with no stated payoffs is fragile in exactly the way the plot will stress it.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Write the payoffs: one paragraph per great power (Seismograph access, Drowndust veto, Gray Market tolerance).
2. Keep it underspecified but mark it: the payoffs are the Compact's best-kept secret (Level-7-adjacent — the Worldtides' silence covers it).
3. Make the underspecification the plot: a great power is *reconsidering* its payoff (the 2024 crisis engine).

### MIL-004 — LOW — The Ninth Bell vs. a mid-size state
**Merges:** MIL-002 (Low)
**Location:** `09_MILITARY.md` (Ninth Bell)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 09: "A T4 can win a firefight; a T4 cannot win a *campaign*" — the Ninth Bell's capability is described in terms implying strategic deterrence.
- 09: the Drowndust doctrine (MIL-002) reserves strategic deterrence to Drowndust — the Bell's role overlaps it.
**Why it is a problem:** Two strategic deterrents with overlapping mandates and no deconfliction.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Deconflict: the Bell is *tactical* (a scalpel), Drowndust is *strategic* (a hammer) — state the division.
2. Merge them: the Bell *is* the Drowndust delivery doctrine's euphemism.
3. Make the overlap the plot: the Bell's custodians and the Drowndust authority are rivals (but that's a new conflict — price it).

### MIL-005 — LOW — The 90-second rule's scope excludes passive detection
**Merges:** MIL-003 (Low)
**Location:** `09_MILITARY.md` (90-second rule)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 09: the 90-second rule governs *response* to Threshold events — "the ninety-second rule" — but passive detection (Seismograph watch, Lantern standby) isn't scoped.
**Why it is a problem:** The rule's edges are where incidents happen: a 91-second passive track with no response protocol is a gap.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Scope it: the rule covers active response; passive tracking follows the Seismograph Authority's standing orders (cite them).
2. Extend the rule to passive detection (but the 90-second number was chosen for response — the passive number should differ).
3. Leave the gap and make it the Saratov lesson (the doctrine's known hole).

### MIL-006 — LOW — The Saratov sleeper's Erosion status is unstated
**Merges:** MIL-006 (Low)
**Location:** `09_MILITARY.md` (Saratov precedent)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 09: "the **Saratov sleeper** (a comatose T5 — see [Governments](06_GOVERNMENTS.md), GOV-003)… Weakness: the deterrent is a Drowning waiting to happen" — the "Dead Hand Quiet" precedent for Drowndust use.
- (Absence): the sleeper's own Erosion/Fathom status after the event is never stated.
**Why it is a problem:** The bible's only worked example of strategic Threshold warfare doesn't say what happened to the operator — the human cost is missing from the lesson.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. State it: the sleeper Drowned (the lesson's price), survived at high Fathoms (the lesson's warning), or vanished (the lesson's mystery).
2. Classify it: the status is sealed (Level-7-adjacent), and the sealing is the point.
3. Leave it as an L4 mystery in 33 (but it currently isn't registered there).

### LAW-003 — LOW — CASE-006's damages cap
**Merges:** LAW-006 (Low)
**Location:** `14_LAW_ENFORCEMENT.md` (CASE-006)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 14: "the court capped damages at the BPF's annual field budget, noting that bankrupting the agency would kill more people than the misclassification did" — but the cap's *basis* (statutory? Tribunal discretion? Compact assessment?) isn't cited.
**Why it is a problem:** An uncited cap is unenforceable in-world: the losing party's counsel would ask "says who?"
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Cite the basis (Tribunal standing order, with date).
2. Make the cap *disputed*: CASE-006 is under appeal on the cap point (live law, not settled law).
3. Remove the cap (but then the case's precedential value changes).

### LAW-004 — LOW — Finders law's reporting duty
**Merges:** LAW-007 (Low)
**Location:** `14_LAW_ENFORCEMENT.md` (finders law)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 14: the finders-law reporting duty for recovered materials ("Unregistered Seep materials are seized, not debated") is stated — but the *penalty* for non-reporting isn't, and the Gray Market's existence implies non-reporting is routine.
**Why it is a problem:** A duty with no penalty is advice. The Gray Market's supply chain runs through this gap.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. State the penalty (and the enforcement rate — the gap between the two is the Gray Market's margin).
2. Make non-reporting the Gray Market's *founding* crime: the market exists because the duty is unenforced (but then say why it's unenforced).
3. Carve out the exception: certain materials (resonant, sub-Threshold) are exempt — the exemption is the laundering path.

### INFO-002 — LOW — Static rot vs. second-order records
**Merges:** INFO-004 (Low)
**Location:** `26_INFORMATION_CONTROL.md` (static rot)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 26: "Static rot is proportional to event intensity" — records of events decay.
- 26: second-order records (records *of* records — catalogs, indexes, the 35 database) aren't addressed: does rot apply transitively?
**Why it is a problem:** The 35 database is a second-order record. If rot is transitive, the database rots; if it isn't, the rot doctrine has a hole.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. State the transitivity rule: rot applies to *records of events*, not to *records of records* (the catalog is safe — but then the catalog's fidelity to rotted primaries is bounded, which should be stated).
2. Make rot transitive (the database needs constant Lantern re-verification — a running cost and a plot engine).
3. Exempt Compact records specifically (the exemption's mechanism becomes a mystery — price it).

### INFO-003 — LOW — Loud editors and the Gray Book
**Merges:** INFO-005 (Low)
**Location:** `26_INFORMATION_CONTROL.md` (Gray Book)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 26: the Gray Book has Loud editors — but the Loud pipeline (32) treats Loud as *recruits*, not publishing staff. The editorial chain's sensitivity is on record: "Three editors read them; two wrote memos; **all six forgot the details within weeks (Veil)**."
**Why it is a problem:** The Gray Book's editorial chain is the information regime's most sensitive appointment process, and it's undescribed.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Describe it: Loud editors are seconded (from where? under what oath? with what compartmentalization?).
2. Make the editorship the Loud pipeline's *destination*: the pipeline exists to staff the Gray Book (but then the pipeline's other outputs need explaining).
3. Remove Loud editors (the Gray Book is Quiet-edited; the Loud readership is passive).

### TECH-004 — LOW — Phone-sensor Seismograph
**Merges:** TECH-004 (Low)
**Location:** `19_TECHNOLOGY.md` · `07_INTERNATIONAL_RELATIONS.md` (Seismograph network)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 19: modern phones carry barometers, magnetometers, and accelerometers — a crowdsourced sensor grid.
- 07: "The Seismograph network works *because* it's a purpose-built, globally synchronized array doing coherent integration — **your phone's magnetometer never had a chance**" — but no file states whether the Compact uses, can't use, or fears the phone grid.
**Why it is a problem:** The cheapest sensor network in history is unmentioned by the institution that needs it most.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. The Compact *uses* it: anonymized phone telemetry feeds the Seismograph (the privacy implications are the story — and the deepfake dividend covers the anomalies).
2. The Compact *can't* use it: phone sensors are too noisy for Threshold discrimination (state the noise floor).
3. The Gray Market uses it first: civilian pressure-tracking apps are the Veil's newest hole (pairs with TECH-001 option 3).

### TECH-005 — LOW — The tech-should-have-solved sweep
**Merges:** TECH-007 (Low)
**Location:** `19_TECHNOLOGY.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 19: several 2024-ordinary technologies (ubiquitous cameras, payment trails, biometric ID) should have collapsed specific concealment problems — the file doesn't sweep for them. The worker's note: "Gray Markets exist precisely in the gap between 'seizable' and 'seized'" — tech *can't* solve the Gray Market, but the file never runs the per-beat check.
**Why it is a problem:** (A standing methodological note, not a single contradiction.) Every concealment beat needs a 2024-tech pass.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Add the sweep as a patch checklist item (per-beat: cameras, payments, biometrics, satellites, LLMs).
2. Write the general principle: the Veil's 2024 posture is *posture*, not invisibility — the Compact manages *interpretation*, not *observation* (the deepfake dividend as doctrine).
3. Pick the three worst beats and fix them; schedule the rest as a second-pass audit.

### ECON-012 — LOW — Depth-pay incentive effects are unpriced
**Merges:** ECON-010 (Minor)
**Location:** `08_ECONOMY.md` (depth pay) · `06_GOVERNMENTS.md` (SMD staffing)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 08: "**Depth pay:** standard +10–20% per 10 Fathoms of Erosion... A 55-Fathom T3 field operator can clear $500k in government service."
- 04: the Erosion doctrine warns against deep work — the pay structure incentivizes exactly it, and the Drowning externality is unpriced.
**Why it is a problem:** The pay structure incentivizes the exact behavior the Erosion doctrine warns against. The bible should say whether the Compact knows.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. The Compact knows and prices it: depth pay carries a mandatory relief-rotation offset (the incentive is hedged).
2. The Compact doesn't know (or won't say): the incentive gradient is a scandal waiting for a journalist (plot engine).
3. Restructure: depth pay is flat, and the Erosion risk is carried by insurance (but then the insurer's book needs the numbers — cf. ECON-001).

### ECON-013 — LOW — Quiet leave and graywater numbers don't reconcile
**Merges:** ECON-011 (Minor)
**Location:** `08_ECONOMY.md` (quiet leave, graywater market)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 08: quiet-leave uptake rates and graywater market volumes imply different workforce sizes — and "an internal audit in 2021 found 11% of volume leaking; the board chose containment over disclosure."
**Why it is a problem:** Two labor-market numbers describe different workforces.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Reconcile to a single workforce figure (mechanical).
2. The graywater market includes non-Quiet participants (the "gray" in graywater — the workforce figures then honestly differ).
3. Mark one series as estimated (in-world statistics are fallible — 26's doctrine covers this).

### ECON-014 — LOW — RC-029's bank run mechanics
**Merges:** ECON-014 (Minor)
**Location:** `31_CONFLICT_ENGINE.md` (RC-029)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 31: RC-029 stages a bank run — but the Compact backstop (08's CANON) should prevent it, and 31 also says "the backstop is a rumor" (cf. ECON-002).
**Why it is a problem:** The bank run and the backstop can't both be true unless the backstop's credibility is the variable.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Make credibility the variable: the backstop exists but isn't *believed* (the run is a confidence crisis, not a solvency crisis — the classic Diamond-Dybvig beat).
2. The backstop doesn't cover this bank (a defined exclusion — state it).
3. Cut the run (but it's the best financial-thriller beat in 31 — prefer 1).

### ECON-015 — LOW — Argent's fractional reserve
**Merges:** ECON-015 (Minor)
**Location:** `08_ECONOMY.md` · `11_CORPORATIONS.md` (Argent)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 08/11: Argent "quietly extends sounding-denominated credit to two threshold agencies" — a fractional-reserve Quiet bank with no stated reserve ratio and no lender of last resort.
**Why it is a problem:** A bank with no stated reserves and no lender of last resort is a confidence trick the bible doesn't acknowledge as one.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. State the ratio and the backstop (the Compact, presumably — which makes Argent too-big-to-fail, a plot fact).
2. Make it a *full*-reserve bank (the conservative Quiet institution — but then its profitability needs explaining).
3. The reserve fraud *is* the story: Argent is insolvent and the run (RC-029) is justified (pairs with ECON-014 option 1).

### ECON-016 — LOW — The sector table sums below the CANON total
**Merges:** ECON-016 (Minor)
**Location:** `08_ECONOMY.md` (sector table vs $140–200B CANON total)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 08: the sector table ("2024 estimates, PROVISIONAL") sums below the $140–200B CANON total — "the real total is 30–50% higher; the informal sector is unmeasurable," but the missing share is never named as a line item.
**Why it is a problem:** The economy's parts don't add up to its whole — the missing share is unaccounted Quiet GDP.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Add the missing line(s): name the unaccounted sectors (Gray Market estimates, in-kind services).
2. Mark the total as estimated and the table as partial (in-world statistics — 26's doctrine).
3. Shrink the total to match the table (but the total is CANON-stated — expensive).

### FAC-012 — LOW — The Alon/Koi flip

**Proposed solutions:**

2. Make the flip in-world: the faction *renamed* itself (a rebrand with a date — but that's lore debt for a copy error; prefer 1).

### FAC-014 — LOW — The Ferrymen's perfect record
**Merges:** FAC-020 (Minor)
**Location:** `12_FACTIONS.md` (Ferrymen)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 12: the Ferrymen's transit record is "zero lost cargoes in seventy years."
- 12 (Compact loss-database, per the worker): "the Ferrymen *claim* seventy years; the Compact's loss-database disagrees; the truth is they lose cargoes and bury the losses better than anyone" — but 12's profile reports the claim as fact.
**Why it is a problem:** A perfect record in a probabilistic world is either a lie or a mystery.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Make it a lie: the record is *reported* perfect (in-world reputation management).
2. Make it a mystery: register *why* it's perfect as an L4 in 33 (the Ferrymen know something about the Tide).

### FAC-015 — LOW — The Salt Road pilgrimages
**Merges:** FAC-021 (Minor)
**Location:** `12_FACTIONS.md` · `21_RELIGION.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 12/21: "**Enemies:** ... the Ninth Bell (SUP-007), whose pilgrimages to ANOMALY-029 the Road *permits* (hospitality) while reporting to the Compact (pragmatism)" — but no file states who *protects* the pilgrimages (a mass Loud movement through Threshold-adjacent territory, "targets *nine* sites simultaneously, including ZONE-007's perimeter (ANOMALY-029)").
**Why it is a problem:** An unprotected pilgrimage is a Veil incident waiting to happen — the bible should say whose problem it is.
**Proposed solutions:**

2. The pilgrimages *are* the Veil incidents: they're managed as recurring cover stories (noise-farming's oldest program).
3. The route avoids Threshold territory (state the routing — the geography then constrains the pilgrimage's meaning).

### FAC-016 — LOW — The Care Axis absolution
**Merges:** FAC-021 (Minor)
**Location:** `12_FACTIONS.md` (Care Axis) · `34_CONFLICT_MATRIX.md` (Care Axis essay)

- 12: the bombings are attributed to the Bell in the faction's own record — the essay's absolution cites no source.

**Proposed solutions:**

2. Mark the absolution as the Care Axis's *claim* (in-world advocacy, not fact) — the Bell's guilt stays open.
3. Register the bombings' attribution as a disputed fact (but that's lore debt for an essay overreach — prefer 1).

### FAC-017 — LOW — The conflict matrix's vocabulary is inconsistent
**Merges:** FAC-022 (Minor) · FAC-023 (Minor) · FAC-024 (Minor)
**Location:** `34_CONFLICT_MATRIX.md`

**Proposed solutions:**

2. Rebuild the matrix after decision (b) (the registry change will force a rebuild anyway — do both at once).

### FAC-018 — LOW — The SMD/Blackwater Syndicate ally+enemy coding
**Merges:** FAC-025 (Minor)

- 06: the relationship is a "written-but-unwritten" truce — cooperation *and* antagonism simultaneously.

### FAC-019 — LOW — The Liwanag Bay Seep leftover
**Merges:** FAC-026 (Minor)
**Location:** `12_FACTIONS.md` · `20_GEOGRAPHY.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 12: a faction entry lists "**Enemies:** [CORP-004](11_CORPORATIONS.md) Meridian Re, now also a courtroom opponent over the Liwanag Bay Seep's taxonomy ([RC-026](31_CONFLICT_ENGINE.md))."
- 20: the seep's post-relocation status is unstated — "the Liwanag Bay Seep" is the pre-relocation name leaking through in 11.
**Why it is a problem:** Relocation residue in a faction profile: the seep's status didn't survive the move.

### FAC-020 — LOW — The Argent Liwanag branch
**Merges:** FAC-027 (Minor)
**Location:** `11_CORPORATIONS.md` (Argent) · `12_FACTIONS.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 11/12: "Argent Vault Bank's Liwanag branch holds sounding-denominated accounts for Gray Market (IND-006) clients..." — a branch described as important in a theater that's now secondary.
**Why it is a problem:** A branch office described as important in a theater that's now secondary.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Downgrade the branch's described importance to match the secondary theater (mechanical).
2. Keep the importance: the Liwanag branch is Argent's *Gray Market* hub (secondary theater, primary function — the mismatch becomes texture).
3. Move the function to a Ravenscroft branch (but the Liwanag branch then needs a stated residual role).

### FAC-021 — LOW — CORP-013 is unprofiled
**Merges:** FAC-028 (Minor)
**Location:** `11_CORPORATIONS.md` (CORP-013) · `35_CANON_DATABASE.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 35: "Ferrymen Mutual" — "Ferrymen's insurance arm; maritime Quiet cover."
- 11: no CORP-013 profile exists — a dangling corporate reference ("Ferrymen Mutual faces an insurance paradox: the voyage is completed before the risk attaches").
**Why it is a problem:** A dangling corporate reference.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Write the profile (short).
2. Remove the references (if the corporation isn't needed).
3. Fold it into an existing corporation as a subsidiary.

### FAC-022 — LOW — The triple go-legitimate beat
**Merges:** FAC-029 (Minor)
**Location:** `12_FACTIONS.md` · `25_TIMEmessaging app.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 12/25: three separate factions are described as "going legitimate" — but the Compact's amnesty/rehabilitation mechanism for criminal factions is never stated.
**Why it is a problem:** Going legitimate is a legal process. Without one, the three beats are handwaves.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Write the mechanism: the Compact's rehabilitation protocol (a real-world parallel: the Italian *pentiti* regime — cite the in-world equivalent).
2. Make the beats aspirational: the factions *claim* to be going legitimate (no one believes them — the skepticism is the story).
3. Cut two of the three (one reformed faction is a beat; three is a trend that needs a mechanism).

### FAC-023 — LOW — The four Okafors
**Merges:** FAC-030 (Minor)
**Location:** `12_FACTIONS.md` · `35_CANON_DATABASE.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 12/35: four characters named Okafor appear across four factions — "no relation — it is a common name, and the hidden world is small enough for coincidence to look like conspiracy" (the worker's note) — but the relation is unstated in canon.
**Why it is a problem:** Four identical surnames with no stated relation is either a family (say so) or a copy error (fix it).
**Proposed solutions:**
1. State the relation: a family (and the family's Quiet significance — four Loud/Attuned in one family is notable).
2. Rename three (if they're unrelated — mechanical).
3. Make it an alias: "Okafor" is a *title* or cutout name (but that's lore debt — prefer 1 or 2).

### FAC-024 — LOW — Halcyon/Bell recruitment direction
**Merges:** FAC-031 (Minor)
**Location:** `12_FACTIONS.md` (Halcyon) · `09_MILITARY.md` (Ninth Bell)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 12: "Halcyon (CORP-001) hires Bell-adjacent talent" — Halcyon's pipeline feeds *into* the company.
- 09: the Ninth Bell's staffing draws *from* Halcyon — "| CORP-001 | Halcyon Dynamics | (B) | Simulation-hypothesis engineers; Bell recruitment target (talent) |" — the direction between the two institutions is inconsistent.
**Why it is a problem:** The personnel pipeline between the PMC and the strategic asset points both ways.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Fix the direction: Halcyon feeds the Bell (the Bell recruits from Halcyon's veterans — the prestige gradient makes sense).
2. The Bell feeds Halcyon (retiring Bell operators join the PMC — the money gradient makes sense).
3. Both: a revolving door (but then say so — the revolving door is itself a Veil risk).

### GEO-005 — LOW — The four-agents problem
**Merges:** GEO-005 (Low)
**Location:** `20_GEOGRAPHY.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 20: "the SMD's (GOV-006) regional field office (which watches the landings with four agents, one boat, and legendary resourcefulness)."
- 06: the field office's stated staffing can't field four agents simultaneously — the geography stages personnel the org chart doesn't have.
**Why it is a problem:** The geography stages personnel the org chart doesn't have.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Fix the staffing: the field office is larger than stated (but the "small elite" framing then needs adjusting).
2. Fix the beat: the four agents come from two offices (the prefectural loan — cf. POL-002 — becomes the mechanism).
3. Make the understaffing the point: the beat *fails* for lack of personnel (but that's a plot change — price it).

### GEO-006 — LOW — The Pale Court buyers
**Merges:** GEO-006 (Low)
**Location:** `20_GEOGRAPHY.md` · `31_CONFLICT_ENGINE.md` (RC-043)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 20: the Pale Court's geographic footprint is described without a Ravenscroft presence.
- 31: RC-043's buyer is "a Pale Court cadet branch" operating in the Ravenscroft market (EVENT-093's inquiry).
**Why it is a problem:** The buyer needs a local address. The geography doesn't give them one.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Give the cadet branch a Ravenscroft address (a gallery, a foundation — the kind of front the Pale Court would use).
2. The buyer operates remotely (but the Laggard inquiry wants *proximity* — the remote buyer needs a local agent, who then needs a name).
3. Change the buyer (but RC-043's "wedding gift" beat is Pale-Court-specific — expensive).

### GEO-007 — LOW — The two hills
**Merges:** GEO-007 (Low)
**Location:** `20_GEOGRAPHY.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 20: "INTL-004 Seismograph — the hill station — the cleanest Tide-pressure readings in the metro" — but a second passage places the Authority's "low-pressure hill reserve — the cleanest baselines inside the densest seep field on the coast" with inconsistent relative positioning (which hill is north of which flips).
**Why it is a problem:** Copy-level geographic inconsistency.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Fix the relative positions (mechanical — pick the passage the zone registry agrees with).
2. Rename one hill (if the two passages describe different hills — but that needs evidence).

### GEO-008 — LOW — The 05:58 train
**Merges:** GEO-008 (Low)
**Location:** `20_GEOGRAPHY.md` · `25_TIMEmessaging app.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 25: "Arthur's train is the first one, 05:58, standing room only with the other night-shifters" — "**06:00 — Shift ends.** Reed Arthur ... clocks out of the Ravenscroft branch of [CORP-011] Nusantara Quiet Assurance ..."
- 20: the station's first-train schedule (per 20's transit section) doesn't include a 05:58 departure.
**Why it is a problem:** A timetabled beat that the timetable contradicts.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Fix the timetable (add the 05:58 — mechanical).
2. Change the beat's time (but the time may be load-bearing for the connection — check).
3. Make it a special service (a Threshold-adjacent charter — but that's lore debt for a timetable; prefer 1).

### GEO-009 — LOW — The Karuizawa hour
**Merges:** GEO-009 (Low)
**Location:** `20_GEOGRAPHY.md` · `25_TIMEmessaging app.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 20: "## LOC-014 — The Hill Archive (Karuizawa, Nagano)" — "...an hour from the listening post by the old rail line."
- (Real-world geography): the intercity rail/local timing from the Ravenscroft area to Karuizawa exceeds an hour.
**Why it is a problem:** Real-world geography check fails — the kind of error a Albion-based reader catches.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Fix the timing to the real schedule (mechanical).
2. Move the destination closer (but Karuizawa's resort character may be load-bearing).
3. Give them a faster mode (but a helicopter for a routine trip needs justifying).

### HIST-004 — LOW — The 1977 revision pointer
**Merges:** HIST-006 (Low)
**Location:** `24_HISTORY.md` (1977 revision)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 24: "the Drowndust ban (prohibited in principle in 1949, made mandatory by the 1977 revision, re-ratified by the 1979 Second Protocols)" — cited repeatedly, but the pointer to the revision's full text/location is missing or inconsistent (cf. "the emergency 1978 revision session").
**Why it is a problem:** The Drowndust ban's mandatory revision (cf. FAC-010, MIL-002) is cited without a citable source.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Add the pointer (mechanical — the revision's location in the archive).
2. Write the revision's summary (if it doesn't exist elsewhere — but check 07 first).

### HIST-005 — LOW — The Fukushima Rule's content drift
**Merges:** RED-021 (Low)
**Location:** `24_HISTORY.md` · `25_TIMEmessaging app.md` · `31_CONFLICT_ENGINE.md` (RC-008)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 24/25: the Fukushima Rule as treaty principle — "some Tide-marks too sacred to exploit."
- 31 (RC-008): the Rule staged as containment doctrine — "some places can't be contained, only managed around" / "the Ravenscroft Doctrine."
**Why it is a problem:** One name, two meanings — the Rule drifts between "law" and "practice" depending on which file is open.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Two-clause reading: the Rule *is* both (treaty read-only principle + exported manage-around doctrine) — state both in 24 (see decision (o)).
2. Rename RC-008's doctrine (but the RC's staging uses the name's weight — expensive).
3. Make the drift in-world: practitioners *misuse* the name, and the misuse is documented (but that's lore debt — prefer 1).

### REL-005 — LOW — The Bell's chronology
**Merges:** REL-004 (Low)
**Location:** `21_RELIGION.md` (the Bell) · `24_HISTORY.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 21: "the nine-bell chronology matches Compact cycle data better than it has any right to. (Fitting past data is what apocalyptic calendars do.)" — "the Bell is mapping pressure from below."
- 24: the dated entries for the same events don't align with 21's religious chronology.
**Why it is a problem:** The faith's history disagrees with the world's history.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Reconcile to 24's dates (the world's timeline is the authority).
2. Keep 21's dates as *sacred* chronology (the faith's calendar differs — but then say so explicitly; sacred vs. secular dating is real-world texture).
3. Register the discrepancy as a historiographical dispute (but that's lore debt for a copy error — prefer 1).

### REL-006 — LOW — The Parisada's venting mechanism is THEORY-tagged
**Merges:** REL-006 (Low)
**Location:** `21_RELIGION.md` · `33_MYSTERIES.md`
**Conflicting statements:**
- 21: the Parisada's venting mechanism is described in operational detail — "It works *often enough* that BPF's Bali office has a standing liaison and a policy of not asking detailed questions."
- 33: the mechanism is STATUS: THEORY — "not even wrong, but the data won't go away" — and the operational detail exceeds what THEORY supports.
**Why it is a problem:** (A watch-note, not a contradiction.) The detail risks being read as CANON by drafters.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Tag the operational passages explicitly as THEORY (mechanical — add the tag where the detail appears).
2. Trim the detail to what the THEORY supports.
3. Promote the mechanism (but promotion needs evidence the bible doesn't have — not recommended).

### SOC-003 — LOW — Ward offices' Quiet functions
**Merges:** SOC-004 (Low)
**Location:** `17_MEDIA_SOCIETY.md` (ward offices)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 17: ward offices have Quiet functions — but "The ward office paperwork, when it's due: residence card renewals, the SSW status stamped in kanji he can half-read, the My Number envelope he files without opening" shows only *civil* administration, while the SMD's Ravenscroft field office remit overlaps without a stated division.
**Why it is a problem:** Two institutions doing the same Quiet work in the same wards with no deconfliction.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. State the division: ward offices handle *civil* Quiet administration (registrations, permits), the SMD handles *operational* matters.
2. The overlap *is* the friction: the ward offices and the field office compete (a new bureaucratic conflict — price it).
3. Remove the ward offices' Quiet functions (but the "Quiet is local" texture is valuable — prefer 1).

### SOC-004 — LOW — Chonaikai (neighborhood associations) as Veil infrastructure
**Merges:** SOC-005 (Low)
**Location:** `17_MEDIA_SOCIETY.md` (neighbourhood association)
**Conflicting statements:**
- 17: "The neighbourhood association as the SMD's eyes — the world's densest informal reporting network at street level" — but the *mechanism* (how a neighborhood association maintains secrecy) is unstated: "The neighbourhood association notice board by the dorm gate posts the disaster-drill schedule and the garbage-sorting rules in dense keigo he photographs and machine-translates, half right."
**Why it is a problem:** The most British social institution in the bible is doing Quiet work by handwave.
**Proposed solutions:**
1. Write the mechanism: the neighbourhood association's *kairanban* (circular notice board) doubles as a low-grade information-control channel (concrete, cultural, cheap).
2. Make it informal: the neighbourhood association aren't infrastructure — they're *terrain* (the Veil works through them, not with them).
3. Cut the claim (but the local-texture loss is real — prefer 1).

# RED TEAM — THE QUIET TIDE (v1.2, Albion-primary)

> Worker: AUD-STORY · Date: 2026-09-19 · Method: read-only hostile-reader attack on 01, 02, 29, 31, 32, 33, 35, 36 (+ targeted greps of 04, 06, 08, 09, 11, 14, 16, 17, 18, 24, 25, 26, 27, 28, 30, 34). No canon file edited. No new lore introduced.
> Classification key: **ANSWERED** = the bible answers it (file/section cited; "(inferential)" where the answer is a direct inference from stated Rules). **PATCH REQUIRED** = genuine gap → formal issue RED-### in §N. **INTENTIONAL MYSTERY** = only where the bible explicitly marks it unknowable AND the unknowability is load-bearing (33's closed L5 set; 26.1 Level 7).

## TRUE TOTALS

| Asked | Answered | Patch-required | Intentional mystery |
|---|---|---|---|
| **570** | **543** | **21** | **6** |

(543 + 21 + 6 = 570. The 21 patch-required map 1:1 to formal issues RED-001..RED-021 in §N. Ten of the 21 overlap issues already filed as NAR-###/SER-### in the companion audits; they are re-issued here per the format requirement with overlap notes, so the parent can dedupe.)

---

## A. METAPHYSICS & HARD RULES (Q-001–Q-060)
